Now the queen is at e5.
White still has the knight at c3 and the queen at e2 (safe because of the pawn at f3).
But Black just took a pawn.
However, White can now play `c3d5`.
If `c3d5`, the queen at e5 must move.
So `f2f3` is still a good move to secure the queen.

Is `e2e3` better?
If `e2e3`, White saves the queen.
Then Black can play `Qxe5` or `Qxc3+`.
`Qxc3+` is a check!
If `e2e3`, and Black plays `Qxc3+`, White must react.
`b2xc3`.
Now White has lost a knight for... nothing? No, White captures the queen.
Wait, if `e2e3`, then `Qxc3+`. White plays `b2xc3`.
White has traded a knight for a queen! That's great for White.
So Black won't play `Qxc3+`.

But if `e2e3`, Black can play `Qxe5`.
Then White's knight at c3 is still under attack by the queen at e5?
Check: e5 to c3. File difference 2, rank difference 2. Yes, it's a diagonal!
So if `e2e3`, and `Qxe5`, the knight at c3 is still attacked.

Let's compare `f2f3` and `e2e3` again.
`f2f3` forces the bishop at g4.
`e2e3` just moves the queen.

Actually, let me consider `c3d5` one more time.
If `c3d5`, then `g4xe2+`.
If `Kxe2`, then White has a knight at d5, but lost the queen.
Wait, is the Black Queen at d4 under attack by the knight at d5?
Yes.
So if `c3d5`, `g4xe2+`, `Kxe2`, now the Black Queen at d4 is under attack.
But White is down a queen for a bishop. Still not worth it.

So `f2f3` is the best move. It neutralizes the bishop and saves the queen.

Final check:
Move: `f2f3`
1. Blocks Bishop at g4 attacking Queen at e2.
2. Attacks Bishop at g4.
3. Prepares `Nxf3` if `g4xf3`.
4. Maintains the pressure of `e5` on `f6`.
5. Keeps the queen on the board.

Wait, can White play `e2g4`?
The list says `e2g4` is legal.
If `e2g4`, White takes the bishop.
Then `d4xg4`. Black takes the queen.
White loses queen for bishop. Not good.

So `f2f3` it is.
